# 4. Phase 9 — complete per-Work budgets and rollups

The human plane notes reinforce the budget direction.

**Important measurement change:** optimize engineering execution around **cost per accepted output**, not merely cost per token. The first two canonical outcome metrics are:

```text
Cost per Accepted Pull Request
Cost per Accepted Requirement
```

Token counts, context size, and model prices are explanatory telemetry; they are not the primary success metric.


```text
total spend matters more than context % alone
soft + hard budgets are desired
budgets apply to cloud APIs AND locally hosted LLM execution
Executive Agents should be allowed larger budget classes
```

## 4.1 Data model

Inspect current partial `WorkBudgetRecord` before redesigning it.

Support, without fabricating unavailable metrics:

```text
Work Item reference
budget status
soft threshold
hard threshold
actual spend/usage
override state
override actor/reason/time
created/updated timestamps
```

The schema should be extensible to distinguish:

```text
cloud API cost
local inference allocated cost/usage
blended/total cost
```

If authoritative local USD-equivalent cost is not yet available, do **not** invent it.

Track the authoritative metric available from LLM Manager and leave a TDR/Future Work entry for incomplete local-cost accounting.

## 4.2 Rollups

Aggregate existing session telemetry into Work-level totals:

```text
cumulative API tokens
estimated cloud cost
session count
model/provider breakdown where available
current budget status
```

Do not double-count session telemetry.

## 4.3 Soft/hard behavior

### Soft threshold

Initially:

```text
semantic event
budget status warning
checkpoint recommendation
human/Executive review signal
possible local-reroute recommendation
```

### Hard threshold

Block **new model execution that would exceed the governed budget** unless an authorized override exists.

Do not terminate an unsafe atomic operation in progress merely because the threshold is crossed.

Keep override auditable.

## 4.4 API/UI

Provide Administrator-facing:

```text
set/update budget
view actual vs budget
view session rollup
override hard limit with reason
```

Worker/Observer behavior follows existing role policy.

## 4.5 Tests

At minimum:

```text
0006 → 0007 migration
fresh install migration
budget create/update
soft threshold
hard threshold
override
rollup across multiple sessions
no double counting
unknown/unavailable cost does not become zero/fabricated
role authorization
```

## 4.6 Deploy

PR → CI → staging/validation → production safe-release.

Then add/update the repository agent findings entry required by the prior plan.

Checkpoint.

# 9. Phase 9B — engineering economics / model-efficiency instrumentation

Implement the first measurable model-comparison framework so future model selection is based on **cost per accepted engineering output**.

## 9B.1 Canonical metrics

At minimum calculate:

```text
Cost per Accepted PR
=
all model/API + attributable local compute cost for the work
/
number of accepted PRs

Cost per Accepted Requirement
=
all model/API + attributable local compute cost for the requirement
/
number of accepted requirements
```

Do not divide away failed attempts. Failed/retried runs remain part of the cost.

Where possible also expose:

```text
Cost per Live-Proven Requirement
Human Review Minutes per Accepted PR
Human Repair Minutes per Accepted PR
Wall-Clock Hours per Accepted PR
First-Pass Acceptance Rate
Rollback/Revert Rate
Follow-up Defect/Rework Cost
```

## 9B.2 Outcome linkage

Every execution session used for software work should be correlatable to:

```text
Work Item
Requirement ID(s)
repository
branch
PR number
commit(s)
model/provider
execution session
cost/usage record
test result
deployment result
acceptance state
```

Do not require one PR = one requirement.

Support:

```text
one PR → multiple requirements
one requirement → multiple PRs
```

through explicit many-to-many correlation.

## 9B.3 Acceptance states

Do not assume "merged" automatically means "accepted".

Use explicit outcome states compatible with later human policy, for example:

```text
OPEN
MERGED
CI_VERIFIED
DEPLOYED
LIVE_VERIFIED
ACCEPTED
REWORK_REQUIRED
REVERTED
```

If the repository currently lacks a human/product acceptance event, record the available objective state and keep `ACCEPTED` nullable/unknown rather than fabricating it.

## 9B.4 Cost capture

Capture/associate:

```text
input tokens
output tokens
cached input tokens if provider exposes them
cumulative API tokens
API dollars
local inference usage
allocated energy/infrastructure cost when available
model/provider
session duration
```

Human time should be supported as a later/optional input:

```text
review_minutes
repair_minutes
```

Do not invent human-time values.

## 9B.5 Task classification

Add a lightweight task category/tag so model economics can eventually be compared by task type:

```text
architecture
backend
frontend
database
infrastructure
debugging
testing
documentation
review
research
```

Use existing work metadata if available rather than creating a parallel taxonomy unnecessarily.

## 9B.6 Model comparison report

Create an internal report/API view that can answer:

```text
For GLM 5.3 Flash:
  total spend
  accepted PRs
  accepted/live-proven requirements
  cost per accepted PR
  cost per accepted requirement
  first-pass acceptance
  rework/rollback count

For another model:
  same metrics
```

Allow filtering by:

```text
date range
repository
task type
model/provider
agent
project
```

## 9B.7 A/B bake-off support

Do not automatically spend heavily on benchmarking.

Add documentation/schema support for controlled comparisons where two or more models receive:

```text
same starting commit
same execution plan
same tool permissions
same acceptance criteria
same time/budget envelope
```

Record all runs, including failures.

The first suggested challenger set for future tests may include:

```text
current default cloud worker
one cheaper challenger
one stronger/higher-cost challenger
```

but **do not hard-code model names into the ACMS economic model**.

## 9B.8 Routing direction

Document as architecture direction, not yet autonomous routing:

> Future model routing should optimize **historical cost per accepted requirement/PR for similar work**, subject to quality, security, latency, and policy constraints.

Raw token price alone should never be the routing objective.

## 9B.9 Tests

Cover:

```text
session → PR correlation
session → requirement correlation
one PR → several requirements
several PRs → one requirement
failed run costs included
unknown acceptance does not become accepted
unknown human time remains unknown
cost rollup has no double counting
model/task filtering
```

Checkpoint.

Run it after\n  ANY container recreation.\n- LLDAP 10.0.20.101:3890, base dc=example,dc=com; groups acms-admin/16\n  (jordatech), acms-workers/17, acms-observers/18.\n\n## Docker/Compose pitfalls (from the live drill — see references file)\n\n- **Single-file bind mounts pin the inode**: swapping the file on disk is\n  invisible to the container; HUP reload then serves stale config forever.\n  nginx now uses a directory mount (`./reverse-proxy:/etc/nginx/conf.d`) and\n  the maintenance template is named `*.conf.template` so the include glob\n  never loads it. Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Pin\n  `ACMS_APP_IMAGE_TAG=<tag>` on EVERY compose up in tooling.\n- **Never command-substitute `compose build`** — its stdout is the build log\n  and pollutes captured variables (`invalid tag`). Send build output to\n  `>&2`; compute the tag, never capture it.\n- **pipefail + `grep -c` false positive**: `grep -c` exits 1 when the count\n  is 0; capture the count into a variable with `|| true`, compare numerically.\n- Recovery from stuck maintenance mode: `up -d --force-recreate\n  reverse-proxy`, then re-pin the app image, then verify via `/version`.\n\n## Bootstrap hop (chicken-and-egg first deploy)\n\nThe first deployment of the release tooling cannot use the tooling. Manual\nhop with identical guarantees: verified backup → stop app → checkout target\nSHA → build SHA-tagged image → migrate → start → validate → write release\nmetadata (mark `mode=manual-bootstrap`). Proven 2026-09-26 (~2 min window).\n\n## Verification hygiene (file-tool corruption lesson)\n\nGround truth for any file change is `git diff` / `py_compile` / `bash -n` —\nnever the tool result echo. Echoes can mislead in BOTH directions (phantom\nerrors and phantom success; this session had mid-write corruption injecting\ngarbage text). For large/critical writes: verify immediately after writing,\nand if corrupted, repair via a python rewrite with assertion pre-checks on\nknown anchor lines, then re-verify with git diff.\n\n## Pointers\n\n- `references/live-drill-2026-09-26.md` — bug-by-bug drill breakdown, exact\n  commands, recovery timeline.\n- `references/hermes-harness-live-tests-2026-09.md` — Hermes 0.17.0 live\n  control-mapping results (pause/resume unsupported, steer/interrupt verified),\n  scratch-profile setup recipe, bridge-to-ACMS wiring.\n- `references/hermes-worker-bringup.md` — the full worker-runtime bring-up recipe\n  (VM clone → Hermes venv install via qga → LLM gateway wiring incl. context caps →\n  api-server platform config shape → systemd unit → verification + traps) as\n  executed on acms-worker-001.\n- Repo docs: `deploy/README.md` (operator runbook), ADR-0009 + ADR-0010 (both\n  Accepted 2026-09-26/27), `docs/HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`, `SPRINT.md`\n  (current slices), handoffs under `docs/handoffs/`.\n\n## Combined slice 3+4 state (deployed 2026-09-27, v0.6.0 @ 85f3c92)\n\n- **ADR-0009 Accepted** (amended: live evidence + explicit deployment-authority\n  policy); allocated in `create_work_item` / `assign_primary`.\n- State dimensions stay separate (plan §5): connectivity is ACMS-derived\n  (UNKNOWN/HEALTHY/STALE/UNREACHABLE from contact recency), `agent_running`\n  is harness-reported tri-state — the UI shows both as independent facts.\n- Heartbeat = complete snapshot (`acms-heartbeat-v1`), unknown fields rejected\n  by the Pydantic model; # AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Memory/Session Offload MVP tests (flight plan Phase 8; ACMS-REQ-055..058).\n2|\n3|The primary new proof: Work → Session 1 + Context Package → handoff/checkpoint\n4|→ Session 1 closes → Session 2 receives a compact reconstructed package →\n5|the SAME Work remains active. Plus: advisory bands, completeness validator,\n6|no-fabrication telemetry.\n7|\"\"\"\n8|from __future__ import annotations\n9|\n10|import uuid\n11|\n12|import pytest\n13|from fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n14|\n15|AUTH = {\"Authorization\": \"Bearer test-admin-token\"}\n16|\n17|\n18|@pytest.fixture()\n19|def client(monkeypatch):\n20|    from acms.main import app\n21|    from acms.settings import get_settings\n22|\n23|    monkeypatch.setattr(get_settings(), \"admin_token\", \"test-admin-token\")\n24|    return TestClient(app)\n25|\n26|\n27|def _mk_work(client) -> str:\n28|    r = client.post(\"/api/v1/work/items\", headers=AUTH,\n29|                    json={\"kind\": \"task\", \"title\": \"Memory offload proof work\"})\n30|    assert r.status_code == 201, r.text\n31|    return r.json()[\"work_item_id\"]\n32|\n33|\n34|def _register_agent(client) -> str:\n35|    r = client.post(\"/api/v1/agents/register\", headers=AUTH, json={\n36|        \"external_registration_id\": f\"miam-00111-hermes-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}\",\n37|        \"display_name\": \"Memory Test Agent\", \"trust_class\": \"internal\",\n38|        \"harness\": \"hermes\", \"bridge_version\": \"0.1\", \"protocol_version\": \"a2a\",\n39|        \"capabilities\": {}})\n40|    assert r.status_code in (200, 201)\n41|    return r.json()[\"agent_id\"]\n42|\n43|\n44|def test_primary_proof_multi_session_same_work(client):\n45|    agent_id = _register_agent(client)\n46|    work_id = _mk_work(client)\n47|    # live assignment keeps the work 'in progress'\n48|    r = client.post(\"/api/v1/work/assignments\", headers=AUTH,\n49|                    json={\"agent_id\": agent_id, \"work_item_id\": work_id})\n50|    assert r.status_code in (200, 201), r.text\n51|    assignment_id = r.json()[\"assignment_id\"]\n52|\n53|    # Session 1 + context package\n54|    pkg = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/context-packages\", headers=AUTH, json={\n55|        \"work_item_id\": work_id, \"policy_version\": \"compact-v1\",\n56|        \"selected\": [{\"type\": \"work_item\", \"id\": work_id}],\n57|        \"estimated_tokens\": 1200,\n58|    }).json()\n59|    s1 = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions\", headers=AUTH, json={\n60|        \"agent_id\": agent_id, \"work_item_id\": work_id,\n61|        \"context_package_id\": pkg[\"context_package_id\"],\n62|        \"harness_session_id\": \"harness-abc\",\n63|    }).json()\n64|    assert s1[\"status\"] == \"OPEN\"\n65|    # telemetry\n66|    s1 = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{s1['session_id']}/telemetry\", headers=AUTH,\n67|                     json={\"current_context_tokens\": 35000, \"max_context_tokens\": 70000,\n68|                           \"cumulative_api_tokens\": 90000, \"estimated_cost_usd\": 0.42}).json()\n69|    assert s1[\"context_utilization_percent\"] == 50.0\n70|    assert s1[\"rotation_advisory\"] == \"MONITOR\"\n71|    # checkpoint (complete)\n72|    cp = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/checkpoints\", headers=AUTH, json={\n73|        \"session_id\": s1[\"session_id\"], \"work_item_id\": work_id, \"reason\": \"rotate\",\n74|        \"summary\": \"Implemented slice; tests green; committing.\",\n75|        \"work_id\": work_id, \"status\": \"in-progress\", \"git_state\": \"branch feat/x @ abc123\",\n76|        \"tests\": \"12/12 passed\", \"deployment\": \"none yet\", \"decisions\": \"used SQLite for tests\",\n77|        \"debt\": \"todo: partitioning\", \"remaining_work\": \"deploy + docs\",\n78|        \"next_action\": \"merge PR\", \"artifacts\": [\"git://sha/abc123\"],\n79|    }).json()\n80|    assert cp[\"complete\"] is True, cp[\"completeness\"]\n81|    # close session 1\n82|    s1c = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{s1['session_id']}/close\", headers=AUTH).json()\n83|    assert s1c[\"status\"] == \"CLOSED\"\n84|    # rotation: session 2 gets a reconstructed compact package\n85|    pkg2 = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/work/{work_id}/rotate\", headers=AUTH, json={\n86|        \"prior_session_id\": s1[\"session_id\"], \"agent_id\": agent_id,\n87|    }).json()\n88|    assert pkg2[\"notes\"].startswith(\"Reconstructed\")\n89|    types = [s[\"type\"] for s in pkg2[\"selected\"]]\n90|    assert \"work_item\" in types and \"assignment\" in types and \"checkpoint\" in types\n91|    # session 2 opens with the package\n92|    s2 = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions\", headers=AUTH, json={\n93|        \"agent_id\": agent_id, \"work_item_id\": work_id,\n94|        \"context_package_id\": pkg2[\"context_package_id\"],\n95|    }).json()\n96|    assert s2[\"status\"] == \"OPEN\"\n97|    # THE INVARIANT: same Work, still an active assignment, both sessions listed\n98|    sessions = client.get(f\"/api/v1/memory/work/{work_id}/sessions\", headers=AUTH).json()\n99|    assert len(sessions) == 2\n100|    statuses = {s[\"status\"] for s in sessions}\n101|    assert statuses == {\"CLOSED\", \"OPEN\"}\n102|    work = client.get(f\"/api/v1/work/items/{work_id}\", headers=AUTH).json()\n103|    assert work[\"status\"].lower() in (\"active\", \"planned\")  # work NOT closed by session rotation\n104|    # assignment still ACTIVE (query by id)\n105|    asg = client.get(f\"/api/v1/work/assignments/{assignment_id}\", headers=AUTH)\n106|    if asg.status_code == 200:\n107|        assert asg.json()[\"status\"] == \"ACTIVE\"\n108|\n109|\n110|def test_completeness_validator_rejects_incomplete(client):\n111|    agent_id = _register_agent(client)\n112|    work_id = _mk_work(client)\n113|    s1 = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions\", headers=AUTH, json={\n114|        \"agent_id\": agent_id, \"work_item_id\": work_id}).json()\n115|    cp = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/checkpoints\", headers=AUTH, json={\n116|        \"session_id\": s1[\"session_id\"], \"summary\": \"partial checkpoint\",\n117|        \"work_id\": work_id, \"status\": \"in-progress\",\n118|        # git_state, tests, deployment, decisions, debt, remaining_work, next_action, artifacts MISSING\n119|    }).json()\n120|    assert cp[\"complete\"] is False\n121|    missing = [k for k, v in cp[\"completeness\"].items() if not v]\n122|    assert \"git_state\" in missing and \"tests\" in missing and \"next_action\" in missing\n123|\n124|\n125|def test_advisory_bands(client):\n126|    agent_id = _register_agent(client)\n127|    s = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions\", headers=AUTH, json={\n128|        \"agent_id\": agent_id}).json()\n129|    sid = s[\"session_id\"]\n130|    t50 = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{sid}/telemetry\", headers=AUTH,\n131|                      json={\"current_context_tokens\": 3500, \"max_context_tokens\": 7000}).json()\n132|    assert t50[\"rotation_advisory\"] == \"MONITOR\"\n133|    t72 = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{sid}/telemetry\", headers=AUTH,\n134|                      json={\"current_context_tokens\": 5040, \"max_context_tokens\": 7000}).json()\n135|    assert t72[\"rotation_advisory\"] == \"CHECKPOINT_RECOMMENDED\"\n136|    t90 = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{sid}/telemetry\", headers=AUTH,\n137|                      json={\"current_context_tokens\": 6300, \"max_context_tokens\": 7000}).json()\n138|    assert t90[\"rotation_advisory\"] == \"ROTATE_RECOMMENDED\"\n139|\n140|\n141|def test_invalid_telemetry_not_fabricated(client):\n142|    agent_id = _register_agent(client)\n143|    s = client.post(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions\", headers=AUTH, json={\n144|        \"agent_id\": agent_id}).json()\n145|    sid = s[\"session_id\"]\n146|    # used > max → INVALID → stays UNKNOWN (null), never fabricated\n147|    t = client.post(f\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/{sid}/telemetry\", headers=AUTH,\n148|                    json={\"current_context_tokens\": 8000, \"max_context_tokens\": 7000}).json()\n149|    assert t[\"context_utilization_percent\"] is None\n150|    assert t[\"rotation_advisory\"] in (None, \"GREEN\")\n151|\n152|\n153|def test_memory_requires_admin(client):\n154|    r = client.get(\"/api/v1/memory/sessions/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000\")\n155|    assert r.status_code == 401\n156|", "total_lines": 155, "file_size": 7335, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"ADR-0007: application startup/imports must not create schema — Alembic owns migrations.\n2|\n3|Runs in a fresh subprocess with a throwaway SQLite URL so that schema creation on\n4|import cannot be masked by earlier tests creating the shared test database.\n5|\"\"\"\n6|\n7|import os\n8|import subprocess\n9|import sys\n10|from pathlib import Path\n11|\n12|PROBE_DB = Path(\"/tmp/acms-no-schema-probe.db\")\n13|\n14|\n15|def test_importing_app_does_not_create_schema():\n16|    if PROBE_DB.exists():\n17|        PROBE_DB.unlink()\n18|    env = {**os.environ, \"ACMS_DATABASE_URL\": f\"sqlite:///{PROBE_DB}\", \"ACMS_ADMIN_TOKEN\": \"x\"}\n19|    result = subprocess.run(\n20|        [sys.executable, \"-c\", \"import acms.main\"],\n21|        env=env,\n22|        capture_output=True,\n23|        text=True,\n24|    )\n25|    assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr\n26|    assert not PROBE_DB.exists(), \"importing acms.main created a database file — startup must not manage schema\"\n27|", "total_lines": 26, "file_size": 885, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"JINT-001 — verify the 0004 migration applies cleanly on PostgreSQL (pgserver pattern from test_postgres_integration).\"\"\"\n2|from __future__ import annotations\n3|\n4|import os\n5|import subprocess\n6|from pathlib import Path\n7|\n8|import pytest\n9|\n10|REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n11|PGDATA = Path(\"/tmp/acms-integration-pgdata\")  # shared with test_postgres_integration\n12|\n13|\n14|def _resolve_pg_url() -> str | None:\n15|    url = os.environ.get(\"ACMS_TEST_POSTGRES_URL\")\n16|    if url:\n17|        return url\n18|    try:\n19|        import pgserver\n20|\n21|        return pgserver.get_server(PGDATA, cleanup_mode=\"delete\").get_uri()\n22|    except Exception:\n23|        return None\n24|\n25|\n26|@pytest.fixture(scope=\"module\")\n27|def pg_url() -> str:\n28|    url = _resolve_pg_url()\n29|    if url is None:\n30|        pytest.skip(\"No PostgreSQL available (set ACMS_TEST_POSTGRES_URL or install pgserver)\")\n31|    return url\n32|\n33|\n34|def test_0004_provisioning_requests_applies(pg_url):\n35|    env = {**os.environ, \"ACMS_DATABASE_URL\": pg_url}\n36|    r = subprocess.run(\n37|        [f\"{REPO}/.venv-acms/bin/alembic\", \"-c\", str(REPO / \"alembic.ini\"),\n38|         \"upgrade\", \"head\"],\n39|        env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=REPO,\n40|    )\n41|    assert r.returncode == 0, f\"alembic upgrade failed:\\n{r.stdout}\\n{r.stderr}\"", "total_lines": 40, "file_size": 1231, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-28T14:14:19.843439+00:00] tool · tool: terminal

{"output": "**Requirement**\n\nSession rotation shall be gated on a verifiable handoff that covers Work ID, status, Git state, tests, deployment, decisions, debt, remaining work, next action, and artifact references.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- A handoff-completeness validator exists and its result is recorded.\n- Rotation remains advisory until policy hardens, but completeness is always measurable.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-058 - Economic execution-session telemetry\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 1\n\n**Requirement**\n\nACMS shall track, separately and without fabrication: current context, max context, utilization, cumulative API tokens, estimated spend, session age, and context growth rate per execution session.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Current-context metrics remain distinct from cumulative API tokens/spend (extends REQ-054).\n- Missing source data is surfaced as UNKNOWN, never estimated into existence.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-059 - Work/session cloud budgets\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 2\n\n**Requirement**\n\nWork Items shall support soft and hard cloud-execution budgets. Soft-threshold crossing triggers checkpoint/review/reroute; hard-threshold crossing blocks NEW cloud execution without an authorized override.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Soft threshold produces a semantic event and an Attention/advisory.\n- Hard threshold never interrupts in-flight atomic work; it blocks new cloud execution.\n- Overrides are explicit and audited.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-060 - Relevant-context selection\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 2\n\n**Requirement**\n\nContext Packages shall select relevant hierarchical context rather than injecting everything; selection policy is versioned.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Packages reference which sources were selected and why (policy/version).\n- Unrelated context is not force-injected into every session.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-061 - Compact upward reporting\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 2\n\n**Requirement**\n\nAgents shall prefer compact semantic summaries plus artifact references over full logs/diffs when reporting to ACMS.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Event summaries are bounded-length semantic statements.\n- Large payloads live as artifact references, not inline event bodies.\n\n====SETTINGS====\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nfrom pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict\n\n\nclass Settings(BaseSettings):\n    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_prefix=\"ACMS_\", env_file=\".env\", extra=\"ignore\")\n\n    admin_token: str = \"change-me\"\n    database_url: str = \"sqlite:///./acms.db\"\n    environment: str = \"development\"\n\n    # Web UI human authentication (plan §6; ADR-0008).\n    # Machine/API access stays on the bearer token and is never shared with the browser.\n    # Empty/placeholder values fail closed: UI login is disabled rather than insecure.\n    session_secret: str = \"\"\n    session_lifetime_minutes: int = 480\n    session_cookie_secure: bool = True\n    ldap_url: str = \"\"\n    ldap_bind_dn: str = \"\"\n    ldap_bind_password: str = \"\"\n    ldap_user_base: str = \"\"\n    ldap_user_filter: str = \"(uid={username})\"\n    ldap_group_admin: str = \"\"\n    ldap_group_worker: str = \"\"\n    ldap_group_observer: str = \"\"\n\n    # Live fleet telemetry thresholds (combined slice 3+4; ADR-0010 Accepted defaults).\n    heartbeat_interval_seconds: int = 60\n    heartbeat_stale_seconds: int = 300\n    stale_reconcile_seconds: int = 3600\n    fleet_reconcile_seconds: int = 86400\n    telemetry_sample_seconds: int = 300\n    session_alignment_grace_seconds: int = 120\n    context_elevated_percent: int = 70\n    context_high_percent: int = 85\n    context_critical_percent: int = 95\n\n    # Agent Bridge targets (plan §15): JSON list of {agent_id, base_url,\n    # api_key}. Unsupported controls are\nadvertised unsupported — never faked (never fake `pause` with `interrupt`).\n\n**Harness under test:** Hermes (Nous Research), **installed version 0.17.0**\n(commit `f3d2dfb`, 2026-06-30) on VM906 (this workstation), profile\n`acms-bridge-test` (scratch profile, no messaging platforms), gateway\napi-server platform on `127.0.0.1:8642` (bearer auth). All tests below were\nexecuted live on 2026-09-26/27 against that instance.\n\n## Capability matrix — Hermes 0.17.0 (api-server transport)\n\n| ACMS capability | Required semantics | Harness mapping (tested) | Tested version | Result |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| status | full current status | `GET /api/sessions/{id}` (id, title, model, message_count, input/output tokens, api_call_count) + `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` (status, last_event, usage, output) + `GET /api/sessions` (last_active per session) | 0.17.0 f3d2dfb | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested) |\n| send_work | dispatch controlled work | `POST /v1/runs` (input, metadata) or `POST /api/sessions/{id}/chat` + `X-Hermes-Session-Id` header for session-bound dispatch; returns run_id/session_id immediately (202 semantics) | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested) |\n| steer | inject direction into active run | `POST /api/sessions/{id}/chat` with `X-Hermes-Session-Id` while run is active — message lands in-session (verified: \"STEER: stop at 10\" → agent acknowledged mid-run \"Stopping there… Summary of what I was doing\") | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested) |\n| pause | resumable suspension of same unit | no HTTP endpoint (`POST /v1/runs/{id}/pause` → 404); only cron-job pause exists (`/api/jobs/{id}/pause`) — different domain | 0.17.0 | **UNSUPPORTED** (404 live) — never fake with interrupt |\n| resume | continue same paused unit | n/a (no pause) | 0.17.0 | **UNSUPPORTED** |\n| interrupt | stop current run/turn, preserve session | `POST /v1/runs/{id}/stop` → `agent.interrupt(\"Stop requested via API\")`; status running→stopping→cancelled; **session preserved** (verified: messages intact, follow-up message in same session answered with full context \"the run was interrupted before any output reached me\") | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested) |\n| cancel | terminal A2A task cancellation | same endpoint as interrupt (`/stop`); the A2A execution task terminates (status `cancelled`); the Hermes *session* survives, so continuing the Work Item later = new execution task bound to the same session | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested; terminal at A2A-task level) |\n| request_handoff | request Markdown handoff | no dedicated endpoint; implemented as a dispatched instruction via `send_work` (\"produce a Markdown handoff…\") with the reply captured from run output/messages | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED (via send_work)** — degrade gracefully if run fails |\n| set_session_title | set work-linked title | `PATCH /api/sessions/{id}` `{\"title\": \"[ACMS-WORK-…] …\"}` (verified live; allowed fields exactly `title`, `end_reason`) | 0.17.0 | **SUPPORTED** (live-tested) |\n\n## Other live-verified facts (plan §14 test log)\n\n| # | Test | Result |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Version/commit | Hermes 0.17.0, commit f3d2dfbec670 (2026-06-30) |\n| 2 | Status before execution | `/api/sessions` + `/v1/runs/{id}` give full inventory |\n| 3 | Dispatch ACMS-keyed task | `POST /v1/runs` with metadata accepted (202, run_id immediate) |\n| 4 | Title + session id | `session_id == run_id` for run submissions; title set via PATCH stuck (\"[ACMS-WORK-000042] Bridge control live test\") |\n| 5 | Steering | Works into a running session via session chat (verified mid-run) |\n| 6 | Stop/interrupt + continue | `/stop` → `cancelled`; same-session follow-up works; agent knows it was interrupted |\n| 7 | True Pause/Resume | **NOT AVAILABLE** at run level (404). `/busy` queue/steer/interrupt exists; `/platform pause` is messaging-only. References, not transcripts.\"\"\"\n417|    from .work_models import AssignmentRecord, WorkItemRecord\n418|\n419|    item = await db.get(WorkItemRecord, work_item_id)\n420|    if item is None:\n421|        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"work item not found\")\n422|    prior_session_id = body.get(\"prior_session_id\")\n423|    if prior_session_id:\n424|        prior = await db.get(ExecutionSessionRecord, prior_session_id)\n425|        if prior is not None and prior.status in (\"OPEN\", \"CHECKPOINTED\"):\n426|            prior.status = \"CLOSED\"\n427|            prior.ended_at = _now()\n428|    # latest checkpoint for the work\n429|    latest_cp = (await db.execute(\n430|        select(SessionCheckpointRecord)\n431|        .where(SessionCheckpointRecord.work_item_id == work_item_id)\n432|        .order_by(SessionCheckpointRecord.created_at.desc()).limit(1)\n433|    )).scalar_one_or_none()\n434|    # [Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

Prod **VM114**\n(10.0.20.108), staging **VM120** (10.0.20.131, both on node `miam-00135`).\nCD: LXC-130 self-hosted runner (staging auto-deploys on main push; prod is a\nmanual release transaction). Do not edit prod files in place.\n\n## Server Manager API (:8300) — auth pattern\n\n- Scoped bearer identities from `/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens`\n  (0600, outside Git). **File format trap:** lines are `service=token:scopes`.\n  `cut -d= -f2` yields `token:scopes` — you MUST split off everything from the\n  first colon or every call 401s. Token staged locally at\n  `~/.sm_service_token_acms` (0600, never echo).\n- Scopes: read = runtime:read, job:read, route:read, usage:read, health:read;\n  write = runtime:write, runtime:destroy, job:submit. 401 first, then 403.\n- Endpoints (v1.0.0 + 2026-09-28 additions): `/api/v1/agent-runtimes[/{id}]`,\n  `/desired-state`, `/agent-runtimes/{id}/reconcile`, `/reconcile`,\n  `/agent-runtimes/{id}/state-sync`, `/api/v1/model-routes[/{route}]`,\n  `/api/v1/hosts`, `/api/v1/usage`, `/api/v1/pdu/*` (health, capabilities,\n  assets, asset power-state, action-plans=DRY-RUN, jobs, audit; actuation\n  env-gated `SERVER_MANAGER_PDU_ACTUATION=1`, `off` refused at SM layer).\n\n## Release transaction to VM114 (proven loop)\n\n1. Local: `bash deploy/build-release.sh . dist` → tar.gz + sha256.\n2. Serve: `python3 -m http.server 8899 --bind 0.0.0.0` from `dist/`\n   (Hermes background terminal); kill it after (`pkill -f 'http.server 8899'`).\n3. Pull on VM114 via qga (see below): curl both files, `mv` them to match the\n   sha256 filename, `sha256sum -c` → CHECKSUM_OK.\n4. Unpack the artifact and **run its OWN deploy/deploy-release.sh** (artifact\n   self-hosts its tooling — tooling fixes ride along; same chicken-and-egg\n   lesson as ACMS).\n5. Long-run: `nohup bash .../deploy-release.sh <tarball> > /tmp/llm-release-txn-<sha>.log 2>&1 &`,\n   then poll `tail` of the log via qga until `==> Release ACCEPTED (<full-sha>)`\n   + `healthcheck PASSED`.\n6. If `server_manager/` code changed: `systemctl restart server-manager-api`\n   (its own systemd unit; NOT restarted by the web-app transaction).\n7. Check `deploy/ ops/` diffs before releasing: if the transaction tooling\n   itself changed, do a same-tip drill first (tooling-mid-flight is unproven).\n8. Verify: `curl -sk https://10.0.20.108/healthz` reports the new git_sha.\n\n## CI / merge discipline\n\n- 6 required checks on PRs: secret-scan, syntax, tests, deps, config, db\n  (+ integration-smoke). Merge only when all pass:\n  `gh pr merge N --squash --delete-branch`.\n- Main pushes trigger `deploy-staging` → VM120 auto-deploy. Verify staging\n  picked up the SHA: `curl -sk https://10.0.20.131/healthz`.\n- **Admin-bypass pitfall:** Jordan's token has admin rights; branch protection\n  does NOT block `git push origin HEAD` from a local `main`. After a squash\n  merge you are left ON main — always `git checkout -b feat/...` BEFORE the\n  next commit, or you bypass review (happened twice 2026-09-28; CI covered it,\n  but it's against repo policy).\n\n## PVE qga exec pattern (how you touch VM114/VM124)\n\n- Helper: `~/work/pve_api.py` (LLDAP bot account, `~/.pve_ldap_bot`). Node\n  locations: **VM114 + VM120 + CT122 on `miam-00135`**; **VM124 on\n  `miam00111`**; VM156 on `miam-00133`. Wrong node → 500 \"qemu-server/N.conf\n  does not exist\".\n- Two-step: `POST /nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec` (returns pid) → sleep\n  → `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` (out-data/err-data/exitcode).\n- Stage scripts as base64 (≤9KB chunks) written to a temp file, then decoded\n  and run. VM114 qga has a known wedge risk on long execs — keep each exec\n  minimal, use nohup + log-poll for long operations.\n\n## ARM reconciler semantics (services/reconciliation.py)\n\n- desired RUNNING: observe → start owned VM once (ownership-verified, bounded\n  retries w/ capped backoff, `recovery_count`) → RUNNING; PVE truth corrects\n  stale DB actuals.\n- desired STOPPED: graceful shutdown once → **bounded convergence wait**\n  (`ARM_RECONCILE_SHUTDOWN_WAIT_S`, default 30s/3s polls). Live lesson: PVE\n  state flips LAG the API ack — an immediate status read still says `running`\n  after an accepted shutdown. Never record nonconvergence without the wait.\n- DESIRED_DESTROYED is API-only; the reconciler NEVER destroys. PVE\n  unreachable → record `last_error`, no state flip on unknown ground truth.\n- Single-replica discipline (TDR-0009): one uvicorn worker; no leader election\n  yet. state-sync health vocabulary: PENDING|SYNCING|VERIFIED|STALE|FAILED.\n\n## llmmanager ORM migration (TDR-0008) — parity-test patterns\n\n- Slice 1 done (hosts/active_hosts/model_registry reflective models + read\n  endpoints). Full 22-table inventory in the 2026-09-28 flight handoff.\n  LiteLLM_* tables are NEVER \"migrated\" (adapter boundary).\n- Parity tests (tests/server_manager/test_orm_parity.py, pgserver embedded):\n  - `schema.sql` carries pg_dump ≥17 `\\restrict`/`\\unrestrict` meta lines —\n    strip lines starting with backslash before `cur.execute(schema)`.\n  - `OWNER TO llmmanager` → create the role first (DO $$ block).\n  - pg_dump leaves `search_path=''` on the session → later unqualified DDL\n    fails \"no schema has been selected\" → `SET search_path = public` (the\n    set_config(..., true) form did NOT stick).\n  - `active_hosts` is created by `service/app/active_deployments.DDL` at\n    service startup, not by schema.sql — apply that DDL in fixtures.\n  - **SQLAlchemy unix-socket URLs:** a socket dir in the netloc\n    (`...@/path:5432/db`) is parsed as the DB path → engine host=None →\n    silently targets `/var/run/postgresql`. `_make_url` now emits socket dirs\n    as `?host=...` — keep it that way.\n- Settings/test-env hygiene: `SERVER_MANAGER_LLM_PG_*` env, then clear\n  `session._engines`, `session._sessions`, `get_settings.cache_clear()`;\n  token-file env must be saved/restored across tests (mtime-keyed cache).\n- FastAPI gotcha: an auth dependency's inner function MUST annotate its param\n  `request: Request` — an unannotated `request` becomes a required QUERY param\n  and every call 422s with `loc: [\"query\",\"request\"]`.\n- Route introspection: `app.routes` shows `_IncludedRouter` wrappers — probe\n  with TestClient instead of counting APIRoutes.\n\n## Pointers\n\n- ACMS counterpart skill: `acms-project-operations` (CT122 release.sh, UI).\n- PDU: `pdu-manager-vm154`; {"content": "1|# TDR-0008 — llmmanager DB ORM migration is staged, not complete\n2|\n3|Status: Accepted (REV4 §6 allows staged/behavior-preserving migration)\n4|\n5|## Context\n6|\n7|The llmmanager database has 20+ public tables + an agentmanager schema, all created by raw\n8|SQL (db/schema/schema.sql) and accessed by psycopg2 in the 2,600-line single-file app.\n9|Migrating all of it to SQLAlchemy ORM in one sprint risks regressions in the revenue-critical\n10|LLM serving path.\n11|\n12|## Decision\n13|\n14|- This sprint migrates ONLY the new agent_runtime_manager database to ORM+Alembic (greenfield).\n15|- LLM Manager machine API reads (model-routes, usage) go through SQLAlchemy Core `text()`\n16|  queries against the EXISTING schema — no schema change, no ORM models for those tables yet.\n17|- Existing write paths stay on the proven psycopg2 app code.\n18|- Follow-up sprints migrate tables group by group (deployments/hosts/model_registry first,\n19|  telemetry second) behind the adapter layer, each with row-count + checksum verification per REV4 §6.\n20|\n21|## Consequences\n22|\n23|- Two persistence styles coexist temporarily in the llmmanager DB (raw SQL + Core reads).\n24|- `server_manager.llm_manager.adapters` isolates the eventual cutover.\n25|", "total_lines": 24, "file_size": 1174, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Adapters re-exporting the existing proven LLM Manager modules (§5 strangler).\n2|\n3|The legacy import paths (service/app/*) remain the live implementations.\n4|Each adapter here re-exports it so new code imports via server_manager.*;\n5|when a module is later moved, only these adapters change.\n6|\"\"\"\n7|from __future__ import annotations\n8|\n9|import importlib\n10|import os\n11|import sys\n12|\n13|_REPO_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))))\n14|_APP_DIR = os.path.join(_REPO_ROOT, \"service\", \"app\")\n15|\n16|\n17|def _reexport(module_name: str, names: list[str]) -> object:\n18|    mod = importlib.import_module(module_name)\n19|    return mod\n20|\n21|\n22|def _ensure_app_on_path() -> None:\n23|    if _APP_DIR not in sys.path:\n24|        sys.path.insert(0, _APP_DIR)\n25|\n26|\n27|_ensure_app_on_path()\n28|\n29|# Lazy adapter objects: attribute access resolves into the legacy module so the\n30|# existing single-file app keeps ownership of its own constants (no import-time\n31|# side effects beyond what the module already performs today).\n32|class _LegacyAdapter:\n33|    def __init__(self, module_name: str):\n34|        self._module_name = module_name\n35|        self._module = None\n36|\n37|    def _load(self):\n38|        if self._module is None:\n39|            _ensure_app_on_path()\n40|            self._module = importlib.import_module(self._module_name)\n41|        return self._module\n42|\n43|    def __getattr__(self, name):\n44|        return getattr(self._load(), name)\n45|\n46|\n47|v011_core = _LegacyAdapter(\"v011_core\")\n48|active_hosts = _LegacyAdapter(\"active_deployments\")\n49|litellm_sync = _LegacyAdapter(\"litellm_sync\")\n50|recovery = _LegacyAdapter(\"v011_recovery\")\n51|spend = _LegacyAdapter(\"spend_guard\")\n52|", "total_lines": 51, "file_size": 1625, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

Usage: pve_api.py <method> <path> [json_body]\nSpecial commands: login, permissions, realms, nodes, resources,\nvmconfig <node> <vmid>, vmstatus <node> <vmid>, raw <METHOD> <path> [json]\"\"\"\nimport sys, json, ssl, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nBASE = \"https://10.0.20.100:8006\"\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context()\nCTX.check_hostname = False\nCTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\nCREDS = os.path.expanduser(\"~/.pve_ldap_bot\")\n\n\ndef load_creds():\n    with open(CREDS) as f:\n        lines = [l.rstrip(\"\\n\") for l in f if l.strip()]\n    return lines[0], lines[1]\n\n\ndef login():\n    user, pw = load_creds()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({\"username\": user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\", \"password\": pw}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + \"/api2/json/access/ticket\", data=data)\n    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=10) as r:\n        d = json.load(r)[\"data\"]\n    return d[\"ticket\"], d[\"CSRFPreventionToken\"], user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\"\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None):\n    ticket, csrf, _ = login()\n    return raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body)\n\n\ndef raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n    data = None\n    if isinstance(body, dict):\n        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n    elif isinstance(body, str):\n        data = body.encode()\n15:def load_creds():\n21:def login():\n30:def api(method, path, body=None):\n35:def raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n56:def main():", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
